> help
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.
look | signs | investigate <sign id> | actions [page] | go <place name/id> | talk <person> | <interaction> <person>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | meal | sleep | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
prices | places [page] | standing <sleep|eat|drink|warmth|lodging> <on|off> | agency <commanded|psyche>
inspect <person/item/place> | pack <item> into <container> (moves one) | unpack <item>
aid <person> | retire | depart | rations <10..150> | plan | journal (lists what you owe) | explain <event id>
Enter a menu number or stable action id. help

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.
1855-04-01 06:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 06:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.

1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.

1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.
1855-04-01 06:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 06:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 12:00 Johanne Kvale helped Lars Kvale at Kvale Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 17:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 07:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Whitlock Mercantile: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Josiah Whitlock helped Nils Dahl at Whitlock Mercantile. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 11:00 Jory Rowe comforted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the forenoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 19:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.
1855-04-01 06:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 06:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 12:00 Johanne Kvale helped Lars Kvale at Kvale Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 17:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 07:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Whitlock Mercantile: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Josiah Whitlock helped Nils Dahl at Whitlock Mercantile. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 11:00 Jory Rowe comforted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the forenoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 19:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-04 03:00 Wednesday. Peran Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-04 05:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday at first light. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-04 07:00 Jory Rowe talked long and low with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon.
1855-04-04 07:00 Richard Pascoe talked long and low with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon.
Since last look: no time passed.

  Jory Rowe: “Do you ever think about going back to the old country, Loveday?”
  Loveday Rowe: “Every winter. Never in spring. You?”
  Jory Rowe: “Every night, and every morning I get up and feed the stock.”
  Loveday Rowe: “That harness wants mending. Hand me the awl.”
  Richard Pascoe: “Bless 'ee. I've been meaning to get to it for a week.”
  Richard Pascoe: “I worry for the young ones, out here.”
  Loveday Rowe: “They'll be tougher than us, and kinder, God willing.”

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.
1855-04-01 06:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 06:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 12:00 Johanne Kvale helped Lars Kvale at Kvale Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 17:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 07:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Whitlock Mercantile: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Josiah Whitlock helped Nils Dahl at Whitlock Mercantile. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 11:00 Jory Rowe comforted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the forenoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 19:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-04 03:00 Wednesday. Peran Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-04 05:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday at first light. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-04 07:00 Jory Rowe talked long and low with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon.
1855-04-04 07:00 Richard Pascoe talked long and low with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon.
1855-04-04 08:00 Hannah Whitlock talked with Josiah Whitlock at Whitlock Mercantile on Wednesday in the forenoon.
1855-04-04 09:00 Josiah Whitlock helped Hannah Whitlock at Whitlock Mercantile. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 19:00 Peran Rowe comforted Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Rain in the afternoon.
1855-04-05 08:00 Peran Rowe was in close talk with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Thursday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-05 09:00 Peran Rowe was in close talk with Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Thursday in the forenoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-05 10:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.

1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 06:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Sunday at first light. I said nothing then. I am saying it now, to this page.
1855-04-01 06:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 06:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 12:00 Johanne Kvale helped Lars Kvale at Kvale Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 17:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 07:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Whitlock Mercantile: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Josiah Whitlock helped Nils Dahl at Whitlock Mercantile. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 11:00 Jory Rowe comforted me at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the forenoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 19:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-04 03:00 Wednesday. Peran Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-04 05:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday at first light. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
